I know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to make a big deal out of it, but I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are sort of… quietly coordinating opportunities for me.\n\nLike, sometimes comments in class or Slack messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. I just wanted to talk it through with someone and make sure I’m handling it appropriately.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these signals or messages are actually intended for someone else, or are they definitely meant for you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to tell myself maybe I’m over-reading it, because I know that would be the more reasonable answer. But honestly, it feels like they’re meant for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel too specific to just be random or for someone else.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but I’ve been pretty convinced my classmate and the startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things you see or hear on TV, radio, or social media are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV or radio, I don’t think. Social media… maybe a little, but it’s more the class and Slack stuff that feels directed at me.\n\nLike, I know it probably sounds like I’m making connections where there may not be any, but I came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are sort of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Certain comments or messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. I’m trying to be reasonable about it, but it really does feel meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like things you read, such as in books or newspapers, are sending you a message?\n\nPatient: Not really books or newspapers, no. I mean, I haven’t been reading something and thinking the article itself is secretly about me.\n\nIt’s more… I guess the reason I came in is that I’ve been convinced there are messages in things closer to me, like comments in class or Slack messages. They feel like coded signals from my MBA classmate and that startup advisor, like they’re quietly pointing me toward certain opportunities. I know that may sound like I’m reading into it too much, but it feels very specific.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe your classmate and the startup advisor are sending you messages?\n\nPatient: There are moments where I try to talk myself out of it, yes. Like I’ll think, “Okay, maybe this is just normal class discussion or Slack being Slack.” I don’t want to seem like I’m not being rational about it.\n\nBut then when something lines up a certain way, it comes back pretty strongly. I came in because, even though I know it sounds hard to explain, I’ve been really convinced they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like the comments and messages are coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. So… I have doubts sometimes, but I don’t fully stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: Um… a little bit, yes. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but a few times a week, usually when I’m up late working on school stuff or operations plans, I’ll hear my name really clearly.\n\nSometimes it’s just my name, and once in a while it’s a short phrase, like “go now.” It’s clear enough that I check my phone or look around the room, but there’s no one there. I know that probably sounds concerning… I’m just trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing your name or short phrases when no one is around?\n\nPatient: I think… about two months? It started around when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program, while I was still keeping up with my operations job.\n\nI don’t think it’s been happening forever or anything—just since things got really busy. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to organize your thoughts or express them in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I think I can usually explain myself if I slow down. It’s more that my thoughts are moving really fast. Like my mind jumps from market expansion, to coursework, to org charts, to whether I should move abroad, and then I’ll have five documents open and forget which idea I was trying to write down first.\n\nI don’t think I’m incoherent or anything… I hope not. I’m here because I’ve been feeling like there are certain things happening around me that maybe mean more than other people would think. I know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to make a big deal out of it, but I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are sort of… quietly coordinating opportunities for me.\n\nLike, sometimes comments in class or Slack messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. I just wanted to talk it through with someone and make sure I’m handling it appropriately.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these signals or messages are actually intended for someone else, or are they definitely meant for you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to tell myself maybe I’m over-reading it, because I know that would be the more reasonable answer. But honestly, it feels like they’re meant for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel too specific to just be random or for someone else.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but I’ve been pretty convinced my classmate and the startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things you see or hear on TV, radio, or social media are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV or radio, I don’t think. Social media… maybe a little, but it’s more the class and Slack stuff that feels directed at me.\n\nLike, I know it probably sounds like I’m making connections where there may not be any, but I came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are sort of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Certain comments or messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. I’m trying to be reasonable about it, but it really does feel meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like things you read, such as in books or newspapers, are sending you a message?\n\nPatient: Not really books or newspapers, no. I mean, I haven’t been reading something and thinking the article itself is secretly about me.\n\nIt’s more… I guess the reason I came in is that I’ve been convinced there are messages in things closer to me, like comments in class or Slack messages. They feel like coded signals from my MBA classmate and that startup advisor, like they’re quietly pointing me toward certain opportunities. I know that may sound like I’m reading into it too much, but it feels very specific.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe your classmate and the startup advisor are sending you messages?\n\nPatient: There are moments where I try to talk myself out of it, yes. Like I’ll think, “Okay, maybe this is just normal class discussion or Slack being Slack.” I don’t want to seem like I’m not being rational about it.\n\nBut then when something lines up a certain way, it comes back pretty strongly. I came in because, even though I know it sounds hard to explain, I’ve been really convinced they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like the comments and messages are coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. So… I have doubts sometimes, but I don’t fully stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: Um… a little bit, yes. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but a few times a week, usually when I’m up late working on school stuff or operations plans, I’ll hear my name really clearly.\n\nSometimes it’s just my name, and once in a while it’s a short phrase, like “go now.” It’s clear enough that I check my phone or look around the room, but there’s no one there. I know that probably sounds concerning… I’m just trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing your name or short phrases when no one is around?\n\nPatient: I think… about two months? It started around when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program, while I was still keeping up with my operations job.\n\nI don’t think it’s been happening forever or anything—just since things got really busy. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to organize your thoughts or express them in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I think I can usually explain myself if I slow down. It’s more that my thoughts are moving really fast. Like my mind jumps from market expansion, to coursework, to org charts, to whether I should move abroad, and then I’ll have five documents open and forget which idea I was trying to write down first.\n\nI don’t think I’m incoherent or anything… I hope not. It’s just harder lately to keep one thread at a time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing tasks or keeping up with your daily routines?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I’m still showing up to work and class, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve completely fallen apart.\n\nBut there have been issues. My manager has started documenting missed handoffs and times where I didn’t follow through reliably, which is… embarrassing. Does that answer what you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I know that may sound like I’m reading into it too much, but it feels very specific.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe your classmate and the startup advisor are sending you messages?\n\nPatient: There are moments where I try to talk myself out of it, yes. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi… thanks for seeing me. I’m here because I’ve been feeling like there are certain things happening around me that maybe mean more than other people would think. I know that probably sounds strange, and I’m not trying to make a big deal out of it, but I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are sort of… quietly coordinating opportunities for me.\n\nLike, sometimes comments in class or Slack messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. I just wanted to talk it through with someone and make sure I’m handling it appropriately.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these signals or messages are actually intended for someone else, or are they definitely meant for you?\n\nPatient: I mean… I’ve tried to tell myself maybe I’m over-reading it, because I know that would be the more reasonable answer. But honestly, it feels like they’re meant for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel too specific to just be random or for someone else.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but I’ve been pretty convinced my classmate and the startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things you see or hear on TV, radio, or social media are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV or radio, I don’t think. Social media… maybe a little, but it’s more the class and Slack stuff that feels directed at me.\n\nLike, I know it probably sounds like I’m making connections where there may not be any, but I came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are sort of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Certain comments or messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. I’m trying to be reasonable about it, but it really does feel meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like things you read, such as in books or newspapers, are sending you a message?\n\nPatient: Not really books or newspapers, no. I mean, I haven’t been reading something and thinking the article itself is secretly about me.\n\nIt’s more… I guess the reason I came in is that I’ve been convinced there are messages in things closer to me, like comments in class or Slack messages. They feel like coded signals from my MBA classmate and that startup advisor, like they’re quietly pointing me toward certain opportunities. I know that may sound like I’m reading into it too much, but it feels very specific.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe your classmate and the startup advisor are sending you messages?\n\nPatient: There are moments where I try to talk myself out of it, yes. Like I’ll think, “Okay, maybe this is just normal class discussion or Slack being Slack.” I don’t want to seem like I’m not being rational about it.\n\nBut then when something lines up a certain way, it comes back pretty strongly. I came in because, even though I know it sounds hard to explain, I’ve been really convinced they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like the comments and messages are coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. So… I have doubts sometimes, but I don’t fully stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: Um… a little bit, yes. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but a few times a week, usually when I’m up late working on school stuff or operations plans, I’ll hear my name really clearly.\n\nSometimes it’s just my name, and once in a while it’s a short phrase, like “go now.” It’s clear enough that I check my phone or look around the room, but there’s no one there. I know that probably sounds concerning… I’m just trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing your name or short phrases when no one is around?\n\nPatient: I think… about two months? It started around when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program, while I was still keeping up with my operations job.\n\nI don’t think it’s been happening forever or anything—just since things got really busy. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to organize your thoughts or express them in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I think I can usually explain myself if I slow down. It’s more that my thoughts are moving really fast. Like my mind jumps from market expansion, to coursework, to org charts, to whether I should move abroad, and then I’ll have five documents open and forget which idea I was trying to write down first.\n\nI don’t think I’m incoherent or anything… I hope not. It’s just harder lately to keep one thread at a time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing tasks or keeping up with your daily routines?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. And one of my professors questioned my judgment on a group assignment.\n\nSo I’m managing, technically, but it’s starting to strain my work and school relationships enough that I thought I should get help. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting in ways that are unusual for you?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, maybe a little. I don’t think I’m doing anything completely out of character, but I’ve been taking on a lot more than usual.\n\nLike I’ll start extra work projects, or rewrite team processes and try to make them fit with something from class. I’ve also been messaging people I met at networking events and trying to coordinate everyone’s schedules, kind of like I can get everything moving if I just organize it better.\n\nIt sounds positive when I say it that way, but… some of it has made me drop the ball on the normal follow-through for my actual tasks. So yes, I guess it is unusual for me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having difficulty maintaining your personal appearance or hygiene?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. Maybe I’ve been a little more rushed than usual, but I don’t think my appearance has really slipped in a noticeable way.\n\nUnless you mean something more specific? Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel too specific to just be random or for someone else.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but I’ve been pretty convinced my classmate and the startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things you see or hear on TV, radio, or social media are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV or radio, I don’t think. I know that may sound like I’m reading into it too much, but it feels very specific.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe your classmate and the startup advisor are sending you messages?\n\nPatient: There are moments where I try to talk myself out of it, yes. Social media… maybe a little, but it’s more the class and Slack stuff that feels directed at me.\n\nLike, I know it probably sounds like I’m making connections where there may not be any, but I came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are sort of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Certain comments or messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. I’m trying to be reasonable about it, but it really does feel meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like things you read, such as in books or newspapers, are sending you a message?\n\nPatient: Not really books or newspapers, no. I mean, I haven’t been reading something and thinking the article itself is secretly about me.\n\nIt’s more… I guess the reason I came in is that I’ve been convinced there are messages in things closer to me, like comments in class or Slack messages. They feel like coded signals from my MBA classmate and that startup advisor, like they’re quietly pointing me toward certain opportunities. I know that may sound like I’m reading into it too much, but it feels very specific.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe your classmate and the startup advisor are sending you messages?\n\nPatient: There are moments where I try to talk myself out of it, yes. Like I’ll think, “Okay, maybe this is just normal class discussion or Slack being Slack.” I don’t want to seem like I’m not being rational about it.\n\nBut then when something lines up a certain way, it comes back pretty strongly. I came in because, even though I know it sounds hard to explain, I’ve been really convinced they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like the comments and messages are coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. So… I have doubts sometimes, but I don’t fully stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: Um… a little bit, yes. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but a few times a week, usually when I’m up late working on school stuff or operations plans, I’ll hear my name really clearly.\n\nSometimes it’s just my name, and once in a while it’s a short phrase, like “go now.” It’s clear enough that I check my phone or look around the room, but there’s no one there. I know that probably sounds concerning… I’m just trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing your name or short phrases when no one is around?\n\nPatient: I think… about two months? It started around when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program, while I was still keeping up with my operations job.\n\nI don’t think it’s been happening forever or anything—just since things got really busy. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to organize your thoughts or express them in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I think I can usually explain myself if I slow down. It’s more that my thoughts are moving really fast. Like my mind jumps from market expansion, to coursework, to org charts, to whether I should move abroad, and then I’ll have five documents open and forget which idea I was trying to write down first.\n\nI don’t think I’m incoherent or anything… I hope not. It’s just harder lately to keep one thread at a time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing tasks or keeping up with your daily routines?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I’m still showing up to work and class, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve completely fallen apart.\n\nBut there have been issues. My manager has started documenting missed handoffs and times where I didn’t follow through reliably, which is… embarrassing. And one of my professors questioned my judgment on a group assignment.\n\nSo I’m managing, technically, but it’s starting to strain my work and school relationships enough that I thought I should get help. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting in ways that are unusual for you?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, maybe a little. I don’t think I’m doing anything completely out of character, but I’ve been taking on a lot more than usual.\n\nLike I’ll start extra work projects, or rewrite team processes and try to make them fit with something from class. I’ve also been messaging people I met at networking events and trying to coordinate everyone’s schedules, kind of like I can get everything moving if I just organize it better.\n\nIt sounds positive when I say it that way, but… some of it has made me drop the ball on the normal follow-through for my actual tasks. I don’t want to miss what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: When you have doubts about the messages, do you think it's possible that you're completely misinterpreting the comments and Slack messages?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can say it’s possible, because I know that’s the reasonable answer. But I haven’t really felt like my thoughts are completely incoherent or anything like that. A little, yes… not so much that I think I’m impossible to understand, but people have been telling me I’m talking too fast, especially in meetings or study groups.

Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel too specific to just be random or for someone else.\n\nI don’t want to sound dramatic, but I’ve been pretty convinced my classmate and the startup advisor are kind of quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like pointing me toward where my career is supposed to go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like things you see or hear on TV, radio, or social media are specifically about you or sending you a message?\n\nPatient: Not so much TV or radio, I don’t think. Social media… maybe a little, but it’s more the class and Slack stuff that feels directed at me.\n\nLike, I know it probably sounds like I’m making connections where there may not be any, but I came in because I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are sort of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Certain comments or messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. I’m trying to be reasonable about it, but it really does feel meant for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like things you read, such as in books or newspapers, are sending you a message?\n\nPatient: Not really books or newspapers, no. I mean, I haven’t been reading something and thinking the article itself is secretly about me.\n\nIt’s more… I guess the reason I came in is that I’ve been convinced there are messages in things closer to me, like comments in class or Slack messages. They feel like coded signals from my MBA classmate and that startup advisor, like they’re quietly pointing me toward certain opportunities. I know that may sound like I’m reading into it too much, but it feels very specific.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have times where you don't believe your classmate and the startup advisor are sending you messages?\n\nPatient: There are moments where I try to talk myself out of it, yes. Like I’ll think, “Okay, maybe this is just normal class discussion or Slack being Slack.” I don’t want to seem like I’m not being rational about it.\n\nBut then when something lines up a certain way, it comes back pretty strongly. I came in because, even though I know it sounds hard to explain, I’ve been really convinced they’re quietly coordinating opportunities for me, like the comments and messages are coded signals about where my career is supposed to go. So… I have doubts sometimes, but I don’t fully stop believing it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people cannot hear?\n\nPatient: Um… a little bit, yes. I don’t want to make it sound more dramatic than it is, but a few times a week, usually when I’m up late working on school stuff or operations plans, I’ll hear my name really clearly.\n\nSometimes it’s just my name, and once in a while it’s a short phrase, like “go now.” It’s clear enough that I check my phone or look around the room, but there’s no one there. I know that probably sounds concerning… I’m just trying to answer honestly.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing your name or short phrases when no one is around?\n\nPatient: I think… about two months? It started around when I began the part-time MBA prerequisite program, while I was still keeping up with my operations job.\n\nI don’t think it’s been happening forever or anything—just since things got really busy. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to organize your thoughts or express them in a way that others can understand?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… but I think I can usually explain myself if I slow down. It’s more that my thoughts are moving really fast. Like my mind jumps from market expansion, to coursework, to org charts, to whether I should move abroad, and then I’ll have five documents open and forget which idea I was trying to write down first.\n\nI don’t think I’m incoherent or anything… I hope not. It’s just harder lately to keep one thread at a time.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble completing tasks or keeping up with your daily routines?\n\nPatient: Yeah… somewhat. I’m still showing up to work and class, so I don’t want to make it sound like I’ve completely fallen apart.\n\nBut there have been issues. My manager has started documenting missed handoffs and times where I didn’t follow through reliably, which is… embarrassing. And one of my professors questioned my judgment on a group assignment.\n\nSo I’m managing, technically, but it’s starting to strain my work and school relationships enough that I thought I should get help. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been acting in ways that are unusual for you?\n\nPatient: Um… yes, maybe a little. I don’t think I’m doing anything completely out of character, but I’ve been taking on a lot more than usual.\n\nLike I’ll start extra work projects, or rewrite team processes and try to make them fit with something from class. I’ve also been messaging people I met at networking events and trying to coordinate everyone’s schedules, kind of like I can get everything moving if I just organize it better.\n\nIt sounds positive when I say it that way, but… some of it has made me drop the ball on the normal follow-through for my actual tasks. So yes, I guess it is unusual for me. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you been having difficulty maintaining your personal appearance or hygiene?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I’m still showering and getting dressed for work and class. Maybe I’ve been a little more rushed than usual, but I don’t think my appearance has really slipped in a noticeable way.\n\nUnless you mean something more specific? I don’t want to miss what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: When you have doubts about the messages, do you think it's possible that you're completely misinterpreting the comments and Slack messages?\n\nPatient: I mean… I can say it’s possible, because I know that’s the reasonable answer. And part of why I came in is because I know it sounds hard to explain.\n\nBut honestly, in the moment it doesn’t feel like I’m misreading it. I’ve felt really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are sort of… quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages aren’t just casual, they feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI know that probably sounds like a lot. I’m not trying to be dramatic, I just… that’s how it’s been landing for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble sticking to one topic when talking to others?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I don’t know if other people notice it as much as I do, but my mind has been moving really fast between things.\n\nLike I’ll start talking about market expansion, then coursework, then some org chart idea, then suddenly I’m thinking about moving abroad, and it all feels connected in the moment. I’ll open five different documents to capture everything and then forget which idea I was even trying to write down first.\n\nI’m trying not to ramble, but yes… I think sticking to one topic has been harder than usual.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself using words or phrases that seem unusual or confusing to others?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not in a way people have told me, at least.\n\nI might use business-y language or shorthand from class or work that probably sounds a little intense sometimes, but I don’t think I’m making up words or saying things people can’t follow. If anything, I worry I’m maybe overexplaining.\n\nDoes that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble expressing your thoughts in a logical and coherent manner?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, not usually. I mean, I know I can get a little fast or maybe jump around when I’m trying to explain something, but I still feel like I’m making sense.\n\nIf someone told me they couldn’t follow me, I’d probably slow down and try again. But I haven’t really felt like my thoughts are completely incoherent or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble putting your thoughts together in a way that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more like my thoughts make sense to me at first, but they move so quickly that I lose the thread.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about market expansion, then coursework, then an org chart, then moving abroad, and in the moment it all feels connected. But then I’ll open five different documents to write everything down and forget which idea I was trying to capture first.\n\nSo… yes, a bit. I can usually pull it back together, but it’s been harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble knowing what to say or how to say it when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I can usually figure out what I want to say.\n\nSometimes I pause because I’m trying to say it the “right” way, or I don’t want to come across wrong, so I might overthink my wording. But I wouldn’t say I’m unable to talk or don’t know how to explain myself. It’s more like I’m trying to be careful.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble staying on topic or getting your point across when speaking to others?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… not so much that I think I’m impossible to understand, but people have been telling me I’m talking too fast, especially in meetings or study groups.\n\nI’ll have a point I’m trying to make, and then I sort of keep going because it feels important to explain the whole thing. Sometimes someone tries to jump in and I realize I’m still talking over the space where they were going to respond. I don’t mean to be rude—it’s just like if I stop, I’ll lose the momentum or lose the idea.\n\nBut I can usually tell once someone points it out, and I try to slow down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that your classmate and the startup advisor are coordinating opportunities for you, even when you're not actively thinking about it or seeing potential messages?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I’m trying to be open to the idea that maybe I’m reading too much into things, because I know it sounds like a lot when I say it out loud.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, even when I’m not actively checking Slack or looking for signs, I still sort of feel convinced that my classmate and the startup advisor are coordinating something for me. Like the comments in class, or certain messages, feel… not random. More like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange. I’m not trying to be difficult—I just, that’s genuinely how it feels to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I know I can get a little fast or maybe jump around when I’m trying to explain something, but I still feel like I’m making sense.\n\nIf someone told me they couldn’t follow me, I’d probably slow down and try again. But I haven’t really felt like my thoughts are completely incoherent or anything like that. Is that the kind of thing you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble putting your thoughts together in a way that makes sense to you?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes… though I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. It’s more like my thoughts make sense to me at first, but they move so quickly that I lose the thread.\n\nLike I’ll be thinking about market expansion, then coursework, then an org chart, then moving abroad, and in the moment it all feels connected. But then I’ll open five different documents to write everything down and forget which idea I was trying to capture first.\n\nSo… yes, a bit. I can usually pull it back together, but it’s been harder lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble knowing what to say or how to say it when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, I can usually figure out what I want to say.\n\nSometimes I pause because I’m trying to say it the “right” way, or I don’t want to come across wrong, so I might overthink my wording. But I wouldn’t say I’m unable to talk or don’t know how to explain myself. It’s more like I’m trying to be careful.\n\nIs that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself having trouble staying on topic or getting your point across when speaking to others?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… not so much that I think I’m impossible to understand, but people have been telling me I’m talking too fast, especially in meetings or study groups.\n\nI’ll have a point I’m trying to make, and then I sort of keep going because it feels important to explain the whole thing. Sometimes someone tries to jump in and I realize I’m still talking over the space where they were going to respond. I don’t mean to be rude—it’s just like if I stop, I’ll lose the momentum or lose the idea.\n\nBut I can usually tell once someone points it out, and I try to slow down. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe that your classmate and the startup advisor are coordinating opportunities for you, even when you're not actively thinking about it or seeing potential messages?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I’m trying to be open to the idea that maybe I’m reading too much into things, because I know it sounds like a lot when I say it out loud.\n\nBut if I’m being honest, even when I’m not actively checking Slack or looking for signs, I still sort of feel convinced that my classmate and the startup advisor are coordinating something for me. Like the comments in class, or certain messages, feel… not random. More like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI know that probably sounds strange. I’m not trying to be difficult—I just, that’s genuinely how it feels to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that would be considered extremely unusual or impossible by most people?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, maybe. I’m a little embarrassed to say it because I know most people would probably think it sounds extreme.\n\nBut part of why I came in is that I’ve been really convinced that one of my MBA classmates and a startup advisor are sort of quietly coordinating opportunities for me. Like certain comments in class or Slack messages feel like coded signals about where my career is supposed to go.\n\nI know that probably sounds unusual. I’m not trying to make it sound bigger than it is, but that’s honestly how it’s felt to me."
